### User · 2026-10-09 06:54:38

Background (from the session that started this one):
Small Talk is a premium press-on nail brand for 18–24 urban desi women; seven sets each tied to a small moment where she shows her hand. The store is being rebuilt on a fresh Shopify Horizon theme; an Opus orchestrator (st-orchestrator) coordinates. The hero shows one fixed finger with a giant main nail, loose accent nails, and a small relaxed hand entering top-right previewing the next set. Existing artwork is rejected: a five-nail preview hand, character crops with rectangular edges, and 9 MB of PNG atlases. Nail designs and packaging are locked and must never be redrawn or recoloured. The owner is running Claude Design separately; character illustrations wait for its Board 1 approval.

Task:
Produce the artwork package for the Small Talk hero and landing page. Work only in C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\artwork\ (create it). Never write to any Shopify theme or product; the orchestrator installs assets. Report to session st-orchestrator by SendMessage (id via mcp__ccd_session_mgmt__list_sessions {"linked": true}), not to the user.

Read first:
- C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\Small-Talk-Claude-Implementation-Checklist.md (WP1 acceptance criteria, defects M4–M7, M10)
- C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\backup\theme-186649313493\campaign-metaobjects.json (set names, colours, nail descriptions)
- Existing atlases in C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\backup\theme-186649313493\atlas-images\ (desi-nail-overlays.png 7×2 signature/accent; desi-nail-accents-v2.png 7×1 extra accent; desi-finger-tones.png 4×1; desi-preview-hand-base.png; desi-preview-hands.png REJECTED five-nail; desi-ink-characters-v2.png REJECTED crops)
- Board 2.jpg in C:\Users\SSTS\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-SSTS-Desktop-Riya\01e61cb4-b351-4d98-a557-f8818be4c4e7\images\ (hero states) and the 14 style references in C:\Users\SSTS\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-SSTS-Desktop-Riya\01e61cb4-b351-4d98-a557-f8818be4c4e7\scratchpad\refs\

Step 0: inventory your image tools (local Python with Pillow if installable via pip, any image-generation MCP such as Canva generate-image, remove-background). Report what you can and cannot do before generating anything. Install Pillow if missing (pip install pillow, run scripts from the artwork folder).

Phase 1 (no board dependency, start immediately):
1. Nail sprites: from desi-nail-overlays.png and desi-nail-accents-v2.png, cut one WebP sprite per set (3 columns: signature, accent, extra accent; identical cell size; cuticle anchor at x=0.5, y=0.925 of the cell), lossless or q≥92, transparent, no fringe. Output artwork/nails/<set-handle>.webp plus artwork/nails/manifest.json with cell size, anchor and per-set column meaning. Preserve exact colours; no recolouring.
2. Finger tones: re-export desi-finger-tones.png as four separate WebP files on an exact common canvas with the nail bed anchor at the same normalised point in all four; record it in the manifest.
3. Preview hand: build a four-visible-nail relaxed hand entering from the upper right, thumb naturally concealed, back of hand with wrist, ~20–24% of a 390px hero width, transparent with clean alpha. Start from desi-preview-hand-base.png if it can be cropped/posed to four fingers; otherwise generate a new one and remove background. Deliver the hand base plus a JSON with the four nail slots (centre, rotation, scale relative to the sprite cell). Test the composite over all seven campaign background colours (#ffb900, #073df1, #e10019, #007948, #ff0078, #b7ef00, #6620f2) and save the seven test PNGs; reject any fringe.
4. Optimised derivatives: every output ≤ 120 KB except the hand base (≤ 200 KB). Record sizes in the manifest.

Phase 2 (WAIT for st-orchestrator to say Board 1 is approved): seven character illustrations as half-face/eye compositions with feathered silhouettes that dissolve into the campaign colour; distinct women; props: a bill, notebook with crescent-bag sketch and blue pen, unbranded silver can, pink rotary phone, dupatta with safety pin, playing cards facing her, two connected hands with correct nails. Then chapter scene art per set.

For every asset save acceptance criteria first (artwork/ACCEPTANCE.md), then inspect the actual output visually (open it) before marking it done; reject wrong anatomy, extra fingers, fringes, baked backgrounds, wrong nail patterns. Keep rejected candidates in artwork/rejected/. Done for Phase 1 = manifest + files + composite tests + a report to st-orchestrator.

### Assistant · 2026-10-09 07:23:05

Phase 1 is done: all 12 image files are in `C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\artwork\` and pass the criteria in `ACCEPTANCE.md`. I inspected every output visually on all seven campaign colours and found no fringe. The report to [the orchestrator](#local_e4896004-68cd-4d55-9d37-ab925783b63b) is queued behind its current turn, so it hasn't been confirmed read yet. The details are also in `st-artwork-phase1.md` in the shared notes folder. `manifest.json` is the single source of truth for files, sizes and anchors.

- **Nails** (`nails/<set-handle>.webp` ×7): three equal cells per file (signature, accent, extra accent), with the cuticle at (0.5, 0.925) in every cell. The extra accents were drawn about 1.56× larger in the old atlas, so I scaled them down to match. All three columns now share one size, and no colours changed. Files are 43–118 KB.
- **Finger tones** (`fingers/tone-1..4.webp`): one 310×840 canvas, with the nail-bed anchor at the same point (0.5, 0.2649) in all four. This fixes M7. At the recommended nail size the tip sticks out about 70 px above the canvas, so the hero container must not clip it.
- **Preview hand** (`hand/preview-hand.webp`, 197 KB, plus `preview-hand.json`): I didn't generate anything. I masked the thumb out of the existing hand photo with a smooth edge, so the anatomy is real. The JSON gives four nail slots (cuticle point, centre, rotation of about −136° to −139°, scale), plus a default nail pattern the orchestrator can override.
- **Size:** all files are within budget and total 927 KB, down from about 9 MB of PNG atlases.
- **Tests:** seven hand composites, each on a set's colour with the next set's nails, plus nail and finger contact sheets and a finger-plus-nail test, all in `tests/`. Two rejected hand attempts are in `rejected/`.
- **Minor flaws:** at 3× zoom, up to 1 px of the natural ring-finger nail can show past its press-on tip. A very faint trace of the thumb crease remains near the new hand edge. Neither shows at display size.

Decision for the orchestrator: how big to show the hand. If "20–24% of a 390 px hero" means the whole image, the nails come out about 8 px wide. I recommend 146 CSS px instead, which makes the four-finger span 22% and the nails about 13 px.

Phase 2 is waiting for the orchestrator to say Board 1 is approved. It also needs an image generator. There isn't one on this machine, and the only option is the Canva connector, which the user has to authorise in their claude.ai connector settings first.

### Assistant · 2026-10-09 07:31:45

I've sent the orchestrator the per-asset reports it asked for: path, dimensions, bytes, anchor and seven-colour contact sheets for each. The orchestrator now has two decisions to make; Phase 2 waits for its Board 1 go.

- **Zero colour change:** the nail and finger masters are now lossless. A check script compared every fully opaque pixel against the source atlases and found no difference for any set (signature and accent columns) or any of the four skin tones. The third nail column (extra accent) was shrunk to match the other two, so it can't be compared pixel for pixel; I offered to keep it at native size instead.
- **Decision 1, size vs exact colour:** lossless breaks the 120 KB limit (nails 118–156 KB, finger tones 201–214 KB). I also made web versions that fit (41–80 KB). Their transparency is identical, but colours shift by about 1 on average and up to 58 at worst (the checks pattern is among the worst). I recommended the lossless set, since only one sprite and one tone load at a time.
- **Decision 2, hand display size:** I recommended 146 CSS px over 78–94 px for the whole image, so the nails come out about 13 px wide.
- **Hand verdict:** a pass, with caveats. At 1× on all seven colours it reads as a back of hand with the thumb tucked under. At 3× the edge where the thumb was is smooth, with no stump and no straight cut. Two flaws remain: a mild change of angle where that edge meets the index finger, and a faint trace of thumb shadow. If the reviewer rejects it, an exact replacement-photo request is ready in the shared notes and I won't keep re-masking.
- **Manifest:** `manifest.json` in the artwork folder now lists both sets of files, the measured colour differences, every anchor and nail slot, and all contact sheets in `tests/`.

I've confirmed I won't use Canva or any outside image tool without the orchestrator's OK. The Diet Cherry can will have no branding when Phase 2 starts; no Phase 1 asset shows a can.

### Assistant · 2026-10-09 08:33:34

I've delivered the Phase 2 readjustment package and reported it to the orchestrator. Several approved assets fail the brief's checks. I only cropped, masked, feathered and composited existing art; nothing was generated, redrawn or recoloured.

I pulled the sources read-only from the store. The Diet Cherry can turned out to be a theme file, not an upload in Files. Each asset has a lossless master plus a web version, all listed under `phase2` in `artwork\manifest.json`. Contact sheets are in `tests\`, and crops for every check are in `tests\checks\`.

**Delivered**
- **Characters** (7): cut from the approved ink sheet. The natural hair outline is untouched; only the straight panel cuts fade out, except on the edges meant to run off-screen as on Board 2. Opaque art pixels are unchanged. Where black hair crosses a cut, the fade looks like a soft dark smudge on yellow and lime.
- **Chapter scenes** (7): one strip per set, plus a version whose top fades to transparent so the page's real campaign colour shows above the scene.
- **Nail guide**: stray specks removed, art unchanged, plus an untouched copy.
- **Shop**: seven finger crops on one shared canvas, and seven product shots of the preview hand wearing each set's own nails.
- **Packaging**: copied as-is, never upscaled, marked "awaiting hi-res from owner".

**Owner conflict**
- The Diet Cherry can file shows a large, legible "Diet Coke" wordmark. The only crop that removes it keeps the bottom of the can and three nails, with a hard top edge that must sit off-screen. I saved that only as a proposal and didn't ship it. The cans in the character and chapter art are plain silver and pass.

**Failed checks (flagged, not fixed)**
- **Chapter scene colours:** none of the seven backgrounds match the metaobject colours. The worst are Pinky Promise (blue channel 98 too low), Diet Cherry (red 70 low) and Brainwave (blue 61 low). The fade version hides most of the mismatch.
- **Chapter scene size:** the strips are only about 270 px wide, so they stay sharp only up to about 135 CSS px. Full-width use would mean upscaling.
- **Chapter scene props:**
  - Pinky Promise has no hands at all.
  - On Me has no bill.
  - Brainwave has no blue pen.
  - The Shaadi Shift safety pin isn't clearly visible.
  - The other props pass, and no nails are visible in any scene.
- **Characters:** all seven are clearly the same woman, so "distinct women" fails. Brainwave shows no crescent-bag sketch. Pinky Promise's hands are clasped rather than hooked at the little finger. They look plausible, but someone should check the fingers, and two nails don't match the set.
- **Finger crops:** Shaadi Shift shows two white-centred flowers where the set has one red-centred flower. Pinky Promise's tip is crimson instead of burgundy. The source runs in a different order from the sets, so I renamed each crop to its set.

I'm standing by for the orchestrator's decisions on the can and the failed checks.

The stacked "small talk" logo should switch from DM Serif Display to Source Serif 4 Black, which is the closest of five candidates to the board 2 header. It's open-licensed and is already the shop headings font, so it adds no new font to ship. The pick and sizing are in `tokens.css` as `--font-logo`. I've sent the sheet path to the orchestrator; the message is queued behind its current turn and not confirmed read.

| Font | Match vs board (with stretch / without) |
|---|---|
| **Source Serif 4 Black** (pick) | 0.684 / 0.632 |
| Abril Fatface | 0.644 / 0.600 |
| Fraunces 900 (the shipped one) | 0.629 / 0.571 |
| DM Serif Display (current) | 0.601 / 0.537 |
| Young Serif | 0.494 |

The score is how much the rendered letters overlap the board's letters (1.0 would be a perfect match). The comparison sheet is `spec\type\compare-logo.png`. Each candidate is drawn at 4× over the board crop, with orange marking board ink it doesn't cover.

- **Settings at 390:** 38.5 px, letter-spacing −0.04em, line-height 0.67, "talk" starting 9.5 px right of "small" so it sits centred underneath, logo blue `#1412d5`.
- **Optional stretch:** the board's letters are about 16% taller than any of these fonts at the same width. I added `--logo-scale-y: 1.16`, which stretches the real text vertically and stays readable to screen readers. Set it to 1 to turn it off. Without it, Source Serif 4 Black still scores best, so the pick doesn't depend on the stretch.

### Assistant · 2026-10-09 15:19:59

I measured the handwritten notes and headline curves on board 3 and sent the values to st-foundation. The message is queued behind its current turn and not confirmed read yet. They're also in `landing.md` under "Measured ink geometry", with the raw data in `spec\landing-inkgeom.json`.

**Notes:** st-foundation guessed right: the board tilts the handwriting much less than the −8° in the spec. The 15–40 px of extra height came from the tilt, not the line spacing, which is already close (39–46 px between lines). Measured tilt per chapter (as a CSS rotation):

| Chapter | Note tilt |
|---|---|
| Diet Cherry | −5° |
| Brainwave | −5.5° |
| Green Flag | −1° |
| Shaadi Shift | −2.5° |
| On Me | −5.5° |
| Wild Card | −4.5° |
| Pinky Promise | −1° |

**Headlines:** the board's curves are flatter than the 420 px radius in the spec. Measured lines range from about 450 to 670 px, and Wild Card's headline is straight. The spec rule is now:
- default radius about 560 px
- about 650 px for lines wider than 300 px
- no curve on Wild Card

Each headline block also tilts by −2° to −6°. This should fix the 12–19 px drop st-foundation saw at the ends of Green Flag, On Me and Shaadi Shift. The radii are approximate (±15%), because the board's lettering is hand-set rather than one exact curve.

**Accepted without changes:** st-foundation's remaining nail differences come from its nails being drawn slightly smaller, centred on the same spot. The name differences on On Me and Wild Card are measuring quirks, so those boxes stay as they are.

### Assistant · 2026-10-09 18:13:52

The shop tile colours and layout are corrected in `spec\tokens.css` and `spec\shop.md`, and I've reported to the orchestrator for st-foundation to build from. Two points need a call from st-fable or the owner: Pinky Promise's tile colour, and that the board shows four loose nails per tile rather than the three in the brief.

**Tile colours:** I took the median of each board-5 tile's background just inside its edge, so the package art doesn't skew it. The values are now `--st-tile-<set>`, and the shop spec uses them instead of the darker hero colours. The raw samples are in `spec\shop-tiles.json`. They have the same hues as st-fable's samples, slightly brighter because the median skips shading.

| Set | Tile colour |
|---|---|
| On Me | `#fcb939` |
| Brainwave | `#0165f8` |
| Green Flag | `#068a43` |
| Shaadi Shift | `#fc2f94` |
| Diet Cherry | `#fd1e36` |
| Wild Card | `#d9e326` |
| Pinky Promise | `#045af1` (flagged) |

Pinky Promise's tile reads cobalt on the board, apparently because the blue of its box art runs to the tile edge. Its campaign colour is violet `#6620f2`.

**Tile layout:**
- **Box:** the package image at 85% of the tile height, 8% in from the left and top, with a CSS shadow.
- **Loose nails:**
  - The board stacks four nails vertically in the right-hand column, not three. Wild Card has three.
  - They're tilted 15–20°, sit a few pixels clear of the box, and the order for each set is listed in `shop.md`.
- **Brainwave, Green Flag and Pinky Promise:** the board draws their boxes almost square, with the box's own nail tray forming the right column. Our package images are portrait and can't fill that space without stretching, so these three tiles get the same layout as the rest: box on the left, a stack of loose nails on the right.
- **Last card on phones:** a single card on the left, about 223 px wide.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 06:01:50

No ChatGPT art has landed yet (`artwork\chatgpt\` doesn't exist), so I built and tested the intake pipeline while waiting. Testing found a problem with the prompt's fallback rule, which I've flagged to the orchestrator as urgent so the fix reaches the owner while they're still generating.

**The problem:** when ChatGPT can't give a transparent background, the prompt tells the owner to accept a flat background in the set colour. That's unsafe for anything we have to cut out:
- In a test where I knew the right answer, Pinky's lilac sweater keyed off violet lost 1.2% of itself, with visible bites out of the edge.
- Across all seven sets, 0.4–12% of the colours in the art sit close enough to their own set colour to be cut away. Green Flag is worst at 12%. A gold nail on mustard or a red nail on cherry red would simply disappear.
- Pure key colours overlap none of the palettes, and the same Pinky test on pure green lost nothing.

I proposed replacement wording for that rule: pure green `#00FF00`, with magenta `#FF00FF` for Green Flag and cyan `#00FFFF` for Wild Card. Product photos keep their set-colour surfaces, because they aren't cut out.

**What's ready for the real files:**
- **Criteria:** written in `ACCEPTANCE.md` under "HD intake".
- **Script:** `artwork\scripts\hd_intake.py`. For each file it:
  - checks for real transparency and rejects painted checkerboards outright
  - cuts out flat backgrounds cleanly and flags halos or bites
  - compares each nail's colour and shape against the existing nail images
  - compares a re-rendered box against the approved one, with enlarged text side by side for a letter-by-letter check
  - checks photo background colours against the requested colour
- **Fits the current build:** HD nails and fingers come out at exactly 2.2× the existing nail and finger images, with the same anchor points. st-foundation can swap files with no layout changes.
- **Output:** files in `artwork\hd\`, contact sheets in `tests\hd\`, and an `hd` group in `manifest.json`. Each file stays "pending-visual" until I've looked at it myself.
- **Test results:** on test files built from existing art, the correct nail matched almost exactly (ΔE00 0.1) and passed; a nail from the wrong column was flagged (ΔE00 28.3). A fake checkerboard was rejected, and the box comparison sheet rendered correctly.

On the model switch: I can't see or change which model this session runs on from here, so I've simply carried on with the task.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 06:30:47

I've processed the first HD batch and reported it to the orchestrator; the report is queued behind its current turn, so it isn't confirmed read yet. Ten pieces are accepted, six Wild Card and Pinky Promise pieces are placed but marked interim while the owner edits them, and the new preview hand is ready with re-measured nail positions. Several pieces are sharper than before but don't reach the 3× sharpness target, because the source files are only 1200×800.

All eight source files had real transparency, so nothing needed cutting out. Edges are clean on the set colour, white and black at 3× zoom. I split each source into the pieces the boards use, fitted each to the board and trimmed anything that would sit off-screen at 390 px. Rebuilding the pages from only the exported files and recorded positions reproduces the boards (`tests\hd\verify-landing-from-manifest.png` and `verify-hero-from-manifest.png`).

**Accepted**

| Piece | Used on | Sharpness at spec size | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| On Me woman | landing + hero half-face | 1.47× | |
| On Me receipt hand | landing | 1.36× | "On me. xx" is legible. All four nails are burgundy, which matches board 3 but not the prompt's mix. |
| Green Flag woman | landing 2.0×, hero 3.1× | | Phone hand nails alternate correctly. |
| Green Flag phone | landing | 2.27× | Separate piece, layered below her. The dial is plain cream rather than gold with numbers. |
| Shaadi Shift eye and safety pin | landing 2.63×, hero 2.64× | | The hero piece is the dupatta and pin only, face off-screen. |
| Brainwave sketchbook | landing | meets 3× | Crescent-bag sketches and a blue pen, no machinery. Replaces the stand-in. |
| Journal tiles (×2) | landing | 2.9× | I removed a baked black outline. |
| Preview hand | hero | meets 3× | Four fingers, thumb hidden, bare nails, so the existing nail images go on it as before. Press-ons sit correctly on the nail beds on all seven colours. Recommended width is 170 CSS px. |

**Interim**
- **Wild Card:**
  - The card backs face the viewer, which is correct.
  - The backs are blue where the board and prompt say red.
  - The woman (short bob, teal top) isn't the board's character.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 07:03:43

I've processed folders A and B and reported to the orchestrator. Every file is identified and 28 assets are accepted. Folder C is still empty, so all 24 product photos and guide images are missing. Pinky Promise is now marked accepted in the manifest, as the orchestrator corrected.

**Identifying the files:** ChatGPT appends a "-N" order number to each download name, which matched `EXPECTED-ORDER.md`. I cross-checked against file times and by eye. Renamed copies are in `artwork\chatgpt\named\`, with the originals untouched; the manifest records each original filename.

- **Folder A (5 files):**
  - The first four are the Wild Card v2 edit, the Brainwave chapter, the Diet Cherry chapter and Our Vision.
  - The fifth is a second Our Vision take; it's visibly softer, so I set it aside and kept the first.
  - Two files' timestamps were in swapped order, so the order numbers and a visual check decided.
- **Folder B (22 of 22):**
  - **Nails:** all 18 accepted. Each matches its set's locked design and colours, almond-shaped and tip up, with clean edges. They're combined into seven sheets at 2.2× the current ones, a straight swap with no CSS changes.
  - **Finger tones:** all four accepted at 1.8×, the most the source files allow without enlarging. The nail sits correctly on the cuticle on all four tones.

**Pieces fitted to the boards (all accepted):**
- **Brainwave:** woman for the landing and hero.
- **Diet Cherry:**
  - Woman, plus the can hand with the board's "Diet Coke" wordmark, which the owner accepted.
  - The new hand comes in from the right, so it can't replace the hero's can (that one comes in from the bottom-left, and flipping would reverse the wordmark). The hero keeps the existing can image.
- **Wild Card v2:** woman and cards hand, separate versions for the landing and hero. She now has the board's long curly hair and lime saree, red card backs face the viewer, and the nails match.
  - My first export left a gap in her saree where the cards had been cut out, plus stray hair inside the cards piece. Both are fixed and checked on a plain background.
  - This replaces the interim Wild Card pieces from batch 1.
- **Our Vision tile:** cropped to the landing tile at 4.8× sharpness.
- **Brainwave pen hand:** exported, but no board slot uses it yet.

**Sharpness:** most pieces land at about 1.8–2.9× sharpness at their board size (only a few reach the 3× target), because the 1536 px sources can't supply more without enlarging.

**Decision for the orchestrator:** with HD files, the hero's first load is 381 KB (largest nail sheet 168 KB, finger tone 170 KB, hand 43 KB), over the roughly 350 KB budget. I recommended compressing slightly more (quality 85), which brings those two files to about 129 KB and 125 KB with a barely measurable colour shift. The alternative is to load the current versions first and swap in HD afterwards.

Everything is recorded in `artwork\manifest.json` under the `hd` group. The check image, rebuilt from the recorded positions next to the boards, is `tests\hd\verify-batch2-from-manifest.png`.

Any `hd/nails` or `hd/fingers` files copied before today need replacing.

- On every hand shot I checked the finger order (index to little, then thumb), and all seven match the prompt.

- **Hero first-load weight:** at the current quality the hero is about 381 KB against a 350 KB budget. I recommended compressing the nail sheet and finger tone slightly more (quality 85, a barely measurable colour change).

  - tab and app icons at 16, 32, 48, 192 and 512 px, with rounded corners

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 10:28:56

The new owner batch is done. I've cut out the five accepted HD boxes and built the logo set from the official wordmark: a clean vector trace, a favicon set, a social share image and the files the owner uploads in Shopify. I didn't touch the held Brainwave and Shaadi Shift boxes. The full file list with checksums went to the orchestrator, and everything is in `artwork\manifest.json` under `hd.batches["owner-D-1"]`.

**Packaging (On Me, Diet Cherry, Green Flag, Wild Card, Pinky Promise)**
- **Cutouts:** each box is cut from the white studio background with clean edges. No white halo shows at 4× on dark, the set colour or white.
- **Floor shadow:** my first pass still included a strip of the baked floor shadow along the bottom. It now cuts at the box's dark bottom edge instead.
- **Files per box:**
  - a full-resolution lossless master (about 865×1150)
  - a product-media file (quality 95, transparent outside the box) for the orchestrator to replace the packaging media on the 5 products
  - web sizes at 240, 360, 480, 720 and full width; 960 would mean enlarging, so it isn't made
- **Sharpness:** those widths reach 3× sharpness on the shop tiles, product page and Our Story.
- **Content:** side by side with the approved 370 px boxes at the same height, the content matches.

**Logo**
- **Colour:** the official wordmark is one flat blue, `#001df9`. That differs from the blue I sampled off the board earlier (`#1412d5`), so the colour token should switch to the official value.
- **Vector version:** the outline trace is accurate to within 0.75 px of the original, with all letters and the holes in both a's correct.
- **Other files:**
  - fallback image sizes for the header and footer
  - a 1200×630 social share image
  - the two files the owner uploads under Settings → Brand: a transparent logo and a square logo on white

**Favicon recommendation:** use the logo's own "s" at browser-tab sizes (16, 32 and 48 px), and the full stacked wordmark for the 180, 192 and 512 px home-screen icons. I tested the full wordmark, the "s", an "s" + "t" pair and the current nail icon on light and dark tabs. The full wordmark can't be read at 16 px and is borderline at 32, "s" + "t" is muddy at 16, and the "s" is crisp. An all-"s" alternate set is there if st-fable prefers one mark throughout, and the nail favicon stays as a fallback.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 12:28:52

I've specced the product-name highlight for the landing chapters and sent the values to the orchestrator; st-foundation builds it and st-fable approves the look. The CSS is in `spec\name-highlight.md`, and the mock of all 7 sets at 390 px (board on the left, proposal on the right) is `artwork\tests\name-highlight-mock.png`.

**Shape (same on all 7):** a highlighter-marker stroke with slightly angled, uneven ends, sitting on a faint oval glow of the same colour. It's CSS only, no new artwork. I chose it because:
- a glow on its own disappears on the light On Me and Wild Card backgrounds
- a straight tilted band reads as a box

The name itself goes up to 26 px bold (from 22 px semibold), in curly quotes.

**Colours and contrast:** I measured contrast on every pixel of the rendered letters, not just the nominal colour pairs. The weakest set still reaches 6.96:1, well above the 4.5:1 minimum.

| Set | Highlight | Text | Lowest contrast |
|---|---|---|---|
| On Me | `#ffe59a` | `#470100` | 13.30 |
| Brainwave | `#dfe7ff` | `#0a2a9e` | 8.92 |
| Diet Cherry | `#ffd6d0` | `#7a0010` | 8.23 |
| Green Flag | `#cdeedb` | `#013a1d` | 8.02 |
| Shaadi Shift | `#ffd3e3` | `#8e0016` | 6.96 |
| Wild Card | `#f2ffbf` | `#0f0b5c` | 16.03 |
| Pinky Promise | `#e4d6ff` | `#5e0412` | 9.22 |

These pairs are now tokens in `tokens.css`.

**One rule st-foundation must keep:** the dark text colours fail against the dark chapter backgrounds on their own (1.4–2.3:1). The name must always render with its highlight stroke behind it and stay on one line. The spec includes a high-contrast fallback for Windows forced-colours mode.

The faint smudge to the left of "Wild Card" in the mock comes from erasing the board's old name; it isn't part of the design.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 17:17:25

The list of existing art st-fable can use to make the laptop landing chapters less predictable is ready in `qa\desktop\owner-v12\art-candidates.md`, and I've sent it to the orchestrator. I made nothing new and didn't touch the theme. The measurements behind it are in `art-inventory.json` alongside.

For every set I listed the approved women, hands, props, HD boxes, product photos, and journal and guide art. Each entry gives its size and the widest it can be shown sharply on a laptop screen. It also says whether it has a transparent background, which edges are hard cuts, and whether the landing already uses it.

What matters most for the composition:
- **Hard cut edges:** cut-out pieces have straight cut edges that must stay on a page or section edge. Moving one inward would show the straight edge, and fixing that needs a faded-edge version I'd have to make (masking only, no redrawing).
- **HD boxes:** all seven are clean cut-outs with no hard edges, so they can float anywhere as a second piece.
- **Opaque photos:** product photos, journal art and guide photos are solid rectangles. They can be framed as a photo card, or faded into the chapter background with a CSS mask. The flat-lay and hand photos are already on a background very close to the set colour, so the fade blends in without any new file.
- **Unused pieces:** a few large, sharp pieces aren't used in any chapter yet:
  - the Diet Cherry hand-with-can (sharp up to about 594 px wide)
  - the Brainwave sketchbook (about 555 px)
  - the tall Shaadi Shift dupatta strip, which can be mirrored
  - the Brainwave pen hand, which isn't on the theme yet and would need uploading

Suggested first try for each set, as a counterweight on the right:

| Set | Suggested piece |
|---|---|
| Diet Cherry | hand-with-can, bleeding off the bottom |
| Brainwave | sketchbook, floating |
| Green Flag | product photo with the pink receiver, faded in |
| Shaadi Shift | dupatta strip, mirrored to bleed off the right |
| On Me | HD box, slightly tilted |
| Wild Card | product photo of cards on felt, faded in |
| Pinky Promise | product photo of the linked little fingers, faded in |

I left out the old chapter scenes (too soft at laptop size) and the earlier cuts that newer versions have replaced.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 18:03:56

I've rebuilt the Wild Card ace and king cards to st-fable's new spec (46×64 SVGs) and handed them to st-foundation for staging. There's no git repo here, so there's no commit or PR.

**Files** in `artwork/hd/wild-card/`:

| File | Size | md5 |
|---|---|---|
| `st-card-ace-spades.svg` | 1437 B | e5fa7e59baadd8477dbc0dccefddc3f2 |
| `st-card-king-hearts.svg` | 2615 B | fe3db249b39c230dc716ce9f838ec21b |

- **Spec match:** both use the requested card size, cream face, rounded corners and thin ink outline. The ace is ink and the king is red, with no shadow; the theme adds it. They replace st-fable's placeholder cards one for one, so the sizes and rotations st-fable already set still apply.
- **Safe to inline:** each card is one rectangle plus one shape, with no ids, `<use>`, `<text>` or `<style>`. Both can sit on the same page or be used as image assets.
- **Design:** the top-left corner shows the letter (A or K) with its suit symbol underneath, repeated upside down bottom-right. The ace has a large spade in the middle. The king has a heart with a small crown, so it doesn't read as an ace of hearts. A plain-heart king is in `alt/st-card-king-hearts-plain.svg` if st-fable prefers it.
- **Checks:** all six criteria I wrote in `ACCEPTANCE.md` before building pass.
  - At the 42 px phone width the corner letter is 9.6 px tall, and the centre art stays clear of both corners.
  - In a real browser, with both cards on one page, they render the same as my own raster, with no holes or id clashes.
  - At the phone and laptop placements on the Wild Card green ground, both corner letters read at 1× and 2×.

  Evidence is in `tests/hd/contact-wild-card-hint-cards-v2.png` and `tests/hd/cards-hint-v2-inline.html`.
- **Bookkeeping:**
  - The manifest lists the new pair under `hd.batches["wild-card-hint-cards"].v2`.
  - Its status stays "proposed" until the orchestrator tells me to flip it.
  - My earlier 250×350 cards are kept for reference but superseded; they don't need installing.
  - The script is `scripts/cards_hint_svg.py`.

I sent the handoff to st-foundation, with a reminder to read back the md5s after upload. I also reported to the orchestrator and confirmed delivery to st-fable. The messages to st-foundation and st-fable are queued until their current turns finish. I wrote no theme files and used no external tools.

**Still open:** st-fable needs to choose the crowned king or the plain-heart one, and the orchestrator decides when the manifest status flips.

- **Background removal:** if the background isn't flat, fall back to a pure #00FF00 key so the pinks and gold survive, with no green or white fringe.
- **Web files:** at native pixels, around 730–810 × 1020 like the other chapter women, never upscaled, with md5s recorded.

import io
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from scipy import ndimage

SRC = r"C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\backup\theme-186649313493\atlas-images"
BGS = ["#ffb900", "#073df1", "#e10019", "#007948", "#ff0078", "#b7ef00", "#6620f2"]
LO, HI = 24, 232  # alpha <= LO -> 0, >= HI -> 255


def load(name):
    return np.array(Image.open(f"{SRC}\\{name}").convert("RGBA"))


def clean(arr, keep="largest", min_area=200):
    """arr: HxWx4 uint8. Returns cleaned copy.
    1. remap alpha (kills faint specks, makes interior exactly opaque)
    2. drop disconnected specks (keep largest component, or all >= min_area)
    3. rim defringe: pixels with alpha < 255 take RGB of nearest opaque pixel
    4. zero RGB where alpha == 0
    """
    a = arr.astype(np.float32)
    al = a[..., 3]
    al = np.clip((al - LO) * 255.0 / (HI - LO), 0, 255)
    lab, n = ndimage.label(al > 0, structure=np.ones((3, 3)))
    if n:
        sizes = ndimage.sum(np.ones_like(al), lab, range(1, n + 1))
        if keep == "largest":
            ok = {int(np.argmax(sizes)) + 1}
        else:
            ok = {i + 1 for i, s in enumerate(sizes) if s >= min_area}
        al[~np.isin(lab, list(ok))] = 0
    out = a.copy()
    out[..., 3] = al
    opaque = al >= 255
    if opaque.any():
        _, (iy, ix) = ndimage.distance_transform_edt(~opaque, return_indices=True)
        rim = (al > 0) & (al < 255)
        out[rim, :3] = a[iy[ri

def srgb_to_lab(rgb):
    c = np.asarray(rgb, float) / 255.0

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 19:00:03

The owner's full-face Shaadi Shift portrait passes all my checks (E-S1 to E-S6) and is ready to install as `st-hd-shaadi-shift-woman.webp`. It's 780×1022 px, the same size as the other chapter women, with md5 b81ba5f8f70f2a9b9960dfa4b46ee62d. No defects found. There's no git repo here, so there's no commit or PR.

**Files**
- **Web file:** `hd/characters/shaadi-shift-woman.web.webp`, 269 KB, q92 like the other chapter women. It's larger than theirs (about 180 KB) because of the dense embroidery.
- **Master:** `hd/characters/shaadi-shift-woman.webp`, 1088×1426 lossless, md5 1ebe1fee0e67400d8518df87dfbdfa2d.

**Clean-up**
- The figure was very slightly see-through (alpha 252–253 instead of fully opaque), so I made it opaque and removed 117 px of invisible specks.
- The dupatta was also cut off by the image's right edge at the bottom. I faded that edge over 64 px so no straight line shows. That matters on phone, and on laptop where the image is mirrored and that edge faces inward.

**Checks**
- **Framing:** cut only on the left edge (which sits on the screen edge) and the bottom (which sits on the section bottom). She's three-quarter, looking right. Mirrored onto the right edge on laptop, she looks toward the copy.
- **Fidelity:** the bindi, both gold jhumkas, the hair bead strands, the gold floral embroidery with its beaded border, and the large gold pin are all there, in the boards' ink style. Colour differences from the reference are dupatta pink ΔE00 1.4, gold 1.4 and skin 4.9.
- **Edges:** no light halo on white, black, pink or the Shaadi ground. The dark line the halo check flagged is the drawn maroon outline of the dupatta. I confirmed that by zooming in on all three backgrounds.
- **Pink on pink:** the dupatta is fairly close to the Shaadi chapter's pink background (ΔE00 7.75), but the outline and gold border keep it clearly separate in the laptop mock.

The contact sheet is `tests/hd/contact-shaadi-shift-woman-E.png`. It has the cut-out on each background, phone and mirrored-laptop mocks, and full-size crops of the eyes, bindi, jhumka, pin, hair edge and the faded corner.

I recorded it in the manifest as batch E with status "proposed". The script is `scripts/hd_batchE.py`. I sent md5s and paths to the orchestrator, st-fable and st-foundation, and told st-foundation to check the md5 after upload. The messages to st-fable and st-foundation are queued until their current turns finish. I didn't make a separate hero crop because no size has been given yet; if st-fable names one, it's a quick crop from the master.
